  1. `peersus.http` `saved` (`{"origin": …}` → `{"saved": true|false}`): whether a usable credential is saved for an origin, by the same read an authorization makes. Nobody is asked, nothing is sent, nothing of the credential comes back.
  2. `peersus.actions` `withhold` (`{"actions": [ids]}`): an extension takes back, for now, actions its own manifest declares. A withheld action is left out of what a window is told the extension contributes and is refused if run (`This action is not available now`). No permission: it can only restrict the caller's own. Kept with the caller's page placements (`page_offers.rs`) and forgotten with them when the extension stops.
- **Fixed (E-087).** An Android window could not send the command that runs an action on the file chosen on Send (it is never written to a frame, and that window reaches its owner by frames). The platform now gives the window an ability (`link::SelectedAction`), which the Android adapter fills with the owner's own in-process client, as it does for shared text; the `cfg(target_os = "android")` branch in the shared controls is gone. A desktop is unchanged (it names a path, in a command any window may send).
- **Updated (the Upload extension, still 0.1.0).** It asks `saved` when it starts, when its settings are opened and after a key is saved or removed, and withholds "Upload selected file" while the answer is no. An upload begins by asking the provider about the key (`check`): a refused key ends it before the file is prepared or the person is asked anything; a provider that could not say does not stop it. Its settings show one line, drawn by the application from a page the extension places in Settings: no key; a key not yet asked about; accepted; refused; with "Check now". The line redraws every two seconds while shown, from what the extension already knows.
- **Found while writing it.** The extension asked every call with a ten-minute deadline. That is allowed only for methods the application declares as waiting for a person; for any other the hub refuses a deadline past its general 30 s before the call is made. `withhold` would have been refused every time, silently. It asks that one with five seconds; a test of the extension holds it under the limit.
- **About "the key is not kept after the application is closed" (owner, 2026-11-15).** Read, not reproduced: on a desktop the key is in the session's secret service under the extension and the address, on Android in a file of the application encrypted with a key of the system's keystore; neither is tied to the running process. What was certainly missing: nothing on screen ever said that a key is saved (the form opens empty every time), and the check that would have said so was the one that never ran. The secret store itself was not looked into, on purpose. If it still happens with the line on screen, it is a different fault and needs the device it happens on.
- **Tests.** The extension's own, 13: the upload is taken back only on a clear "no"; saving asks the provider and removing asks nobody; an upload asks about the key before the file; the settings line. The application's: the withheld set (unit), and a real extension process that takes back one of its actions through the real owner and hub (`tests/plugins.rs`): the window no longer lists it, a press is refused, another extension's action of the same name is untouched.
- **Found by the two by-hand tests that run the built extension** (the interpreter's with scripted answers, the application's against a local TLS server; both adapted to the calls above). Both failed the same way: an upload asked for the moment the extension had started was answered "An upload is already active". The extension refused as busy every call that reached it while it waited for an answer of its own, and its first question (`saved`, before it serves anything) is such a wait: a press made in that moment was refused with a sentence that is not true. **Fixed in the extension:** calls that arrive during that first question are kept (four at most) and served right after it, in order; once started, a call in the middle of another is refused as before. One test of the extension (14 now); both by-hand tests pass with the rebuilt module, and the network crate's tests pass whole after the pairing change.
- **Not verified:** anything on a device; the cost of the two-second redraw (not measured); an application older than this with this extension (expected not to start it, not tried).

### E-091 — Every HTTPS request of the Android engine failed: the Kotlin library the certificate checker calls was not in the APK — FOUND on NX, FIXED in the Android build; the failure of an HTTPS request now says which way it failed

- **Seen on NX (the APK and the Upload extension of 15:5x).** With a made-up key saved, the Upload settings line said the provider "has not been asked yet" and "Check now" changed nothing visible. The application had answered the check with "HTTPS failed; no retry was attempted" within 1.4 s; the phone reaches the provider, and the provider's chain verifies from the workstation (Google Trust Services, valid). The extension showed no reason, and the application's one sentence gave none either.
- **Changed (application, `file_http.rs`).** A failed HTTPS request is told apart in fixed sentences, which name nothing of the address, the key or any answer: no answer in time; the address could not be reached; its certificate is from an issuer this device does not trust, outside its dates, for another name, or otherwise refused; this device's system gave no trust store; the system's certificate checker could not be called; another TLS refusal. The rustls error is found inside the error chain also when it is wrapped twice in `io::Error` (seen: Other → InvalidData → rustls), which `source()` alone does not show. `rustls` is named in the workspace (the copy already in the build, no feature added). One test with a closed port and a server that answers in plain text.
- **Changed (Upload extension, 0.1.0).** It shows on its settings line why the provider could not be asked (the application's sentence, or the status the provider answered with). Its settings line, redrawn every two seconds, is answered from what is known while another call of the extension waits (for the person or the provider): before, each redraw in that moment was refused as busy and the application said "An upload is already active". 14 tests of the extension.
- **Found with them.** The sentence on NX was "the system's certificate checker could not be called". The verifier's Java half (`org.rustls:rustls-platform-verifier:0.2.0`) is compiled Kotlin and calls `kotlin.jvm.internal.Intrinsics`, `kotlin.LazyKt`, `kotlin.Pair` and others; its POM declares no dependency, and this application has no Kotlin of its own. The dex tool counted ZERO Kotlin classes in the APK while the verifier's classes were there: the first certificate check throws NoClassDefFoundError inside the verifier, which reports only that the call failed. So every HTTPS request made by the Android engine (the key check, an upload, and by the same path anything else that uses this client) failed on every phone. **Fixed:** `kotlin-stdlib` 2.2.10 (the version of the verifier's own Android build) is a dependency of the Android application.
- **Owner's decision (2026-11-15):** Upload is put aside until he has updated the provider's site and laid a base; it is to become several providers to choose from. Nothing more is done on it now.
- **Verified on NX after the fix (APK with the Kotlin library; the dex tool now finds `kotlin.LazyKt`, `kotlin.Pair`, `kotlin.jvm.internal.Intrinsics`):** "Check now" with the same made-up key answers in 1.3 s "An API key is saved, but the provider does not accept it." — the provider was reached over HTTPS from the phone and answered 401. Not tried: an upload (paused by the owner), the store and update paths on a phone (same client, expected fixed by the same line, not seen).

### E-092 — Toolkit moved to Freya 0.6.0-alpha.1 (owner's request of 2026-11-15) — IN PROGRESS

- **Asked by the owner:** move to Freya 0.6.0-alpha.1 for its Android and desktop fixes, and keep every crate current from now on. The contract (AGENTS.md) named the 0.5 line; it is updated with this move.
- **What 0.6.0-alpha.1 brings that this application meets (its release notes):** touch releases captured while a scroll view is dragged (#2487); a cancelled touch handled as a release (#2490); pointer-over on touch start and enter on touch move (#2489, #2492); queued events no longer dropped when their node is gone (#2485); buffered mouse moves coalesced (#2472); faster event draining (#2463); renderer bindings 0.105.0-beta.1 on Skia m156.5. winit stays 0.30.13, accesskit_winit 0.34.
- **Not in it:** the scroll-view drag ownership the owner's phones need (#2538) is still open upstream; the maintainer wrote on 2026-11-13 he would push our addition into it. The local copy stays and is carried onto 0.6.
- **Carried (vendored copies, each with its PATCHES.md):** `freya-core`, `freya-components` and `freya-winit` re-made from the 0.6.0-alpha.1 sources with this project's changes re-applied as a patch: offsets only, except four places merged by hand where upstream had changed the same lines (the shared edit callback of `input.rs`, as the field's value became optional; the release, move and press of a touch drag in both scroll views, where upstream had only renamed `set` to `set_if_modified` on the state the copy replaces). `freya-skia-bindings` re-made from 0.105.0-beta.1 with the Fontconfig fix: both texts it replaces are present exactly once in m156.5's font manager.
- **So far:** the lock moved only the toolkit's own crates (and `hotpath`, which it pulls). Skia m156.5 was fetched and built from source with the Fontconfig fix applied (the build's own step, which stops on an unexpected source). The interface crate compiled after one fix in the carried edit callback (the value is borrowed, as upstream does, not moved out of the callback). Then `rfd` 0.18 (owner, 2026-11-15) in the workspace and in the vendored `freya-winit` (noted in its `PATCHES.md`), so one copy is built; 0.18 changes no API used here.
- **Verified (2026-11-16):** workspace lint clean with both feature sets; the interface's 706 tests pass, the toolkit's scroll-view tests among them (run through `peersus-ui`). The development desktop build and the debuggable APK were rebuilt (the Android log shows every `freya-*` crate at 0.6.0-alpha.1 and the bindings at 0.105.0-beta.1 compiled for it) and the APK installed on NX (byte-for-byte). On NX: the engine answers 0.5 s after launch, no error said, the extensions as before; the Send page scrolls under a drag; Settings opens; its list follows a slow drag and keeps going after a flick; a vertical drag that starts on the horizontal row of languages moves the page (the carried drag-ownership rule). As before 0.6, a touch of zero duration injected by `adb input tap` does not press the header's settings button and one of 90 ms does; whether a real finger's quick tap behaves differently was not tried. Not seen: the desktop window on 0.6 (the owner's MSI runs his own build), ROG, HP.

### E-093 — Dependencies kept current by CI (owner's request of 2026-11-15) — IN CODE; scripts tested locally; the workflows run only once published

- **Asked by the owner:** a CI bot that says when there are updates, alpha and beta included; Dependabot's pull requests squashed into one commit on a branch `dev`, made in his name; updates done by CI, by hand only for this project's own patches and personal hotfixes; publishing to crates.io only on his command, never automatic.
- **Added.** `dependency-news.yml` + `scripts/dependency-news.py`: one issue kept current with every dependency newer than what the lockfile resolved (pre-releases included) and every vendored copy whose upstream moved. `dependency-roll.yml` + `scripts/dependency-roll.py`: every open Dependabot pull request for `main`/`dev` squashed onto `dev`, oldest first, one that does not apply cleanly left out and named; one commit authored and committed as `DEV_COMMIT_NAME` / `DEV_COMMIT_EMAIL` (repository variables; nothing is done without them); pushed without force; the gate started on `dev`. `.github/dependabot.yml`: each ecosystem one grouped pull request (majors included) against `dev`. Both scripts' tests are in the metadata gate.
- **Tests.** The report: SemVer precedence of pre-releases, the lockfile's version against a short requirement, vendored copies, a registry that does not answer (4). The roll against a local repository that is only fetched from (this workstation refuses pushes, `--no-verify` and identity overrides, so the test does none of them): two pull requests folded into one commit with the identity given, the conflicting one named, the extension's not folded, nothing made when nothing applies, the push never forced, the gate started, nothing without the identity (4).
- **The report's first run on this tree (live registry):** behind are `keyring` 3.6.3 → 4.2.0, `object` 0.40 → 0.41, `rpassword` 7.5.4 → 7.5.5, `win32job` 2.0.3 → 2.1.0, `x11rb` 0.13 → 0.14, `toml`, `flate2`, `rand` (compatible), and pre-releases `winit` 0.31.0-beta.4, `rustls` 0.24.0-dev.3; vendored copies behind: atspi (3), keyring, netwatch, sctk-adwaita, winit, zbus-lockstep (2).
- **Not done:** nothing published; the repository variables are the owner's to set; no hosted run seen.

### E-094 — About credits and NOTICE laid out for a phone; the Overview says the version and where updates stand — VERIFIED on NX and ROG

- **Seen before** (`shots/rog_about_license_column.png`, `shots/nx_about_0..5.png`): beside a long crate name the licence column was squeezed to a letter a line; the NOTICE kept the hard line breaks of its file.
- **Changed.** `peersus-ui/build.rs` reads each credited crate's resolved version from `Cargo.lock` (and Skia's milestone) into the build; each credit row is now its title `name · version` on one line, then the licence under it, then the text. A title wider than the row glides sideways after a pause and back (`sliding_line.rs`: a pause of 1.6 s, 32 px/s, eased; stopped by a touch or a wheel, still with reduced motion or off screen). The NOTICE is shown as paragraphs (`about::paragraphs`), its words unchanged. The Overview's first card has two rows more: **Version** (the running version the owner names, or the package's) and **Updates** (one word per update state, "not in this build" where the build has none), in the 11 languages; German uses "Updates", since "Aktualisierungen" broke inside the word at the narrowest size (caught by the layout test).
- **Tests.** Versions match the lockfile; FreeType and RustCrypto have none; NOTICE paragraphs keep every word; the glide's pure motion (3); every update state has a translated word and the running version falls back to the package's. The interface's 712 tests pass; its lint is clean.
- **Seen.** Debuggable APK built and installed on NX and ROG (byte-for-byte). On both: the Overview shows `Version 0.1.0 (build 3)` and `Updates not in this build` (`shots/nx_overview_version_updates.png`, `shots/rog_overview_version_updates.png`). On NX: the NOTICE in paragraphs (`shots/nx_about_notice_paragraphs.png`), credit rows title then licence (`shots/nx_about_credit_rows.png`), and `rustls-platform-verifier-android · 0.2.0` caught mid-glide. The owner checked scrolling himself and found it right.

No data is removed.

### E-096 — Notifications, step 4, Android: what reaches outside is a system notification, answered from it — INSTALLED on NX and ROG (byte-for-byte); NX starts and goes to the background with no crash; a notification posted from outside NOT yet seen

- **Changed (Android adapter).** The background loop of the engine (`peersus-ui-android` `protect_transfers`) gives Java the cards the window would draw for the notifications that reach outside (`view::outside_cards`, the application's own words put in the person's language), as JSON, whenever they change. `PostedNotifications.java` shows each as the system's notification (tagged with the owner's number, channel "Notices" in the 12 languages of the resources) while the window is not in front, takes them away when it comes forward or when the owner drops them, and has the sender's buttons. A button answers with its id and swiping it away closes it, both through `NotificationActions` → `notificationAnswered` → `answer-notification`, the window's own command; the owner checks the number and the button. `bridge.rs` has one lookup of the service-or-activity (`background_object`) for the two calls that make their own Java text.
- **Seen:** the Android build compiles; the APK installed on both phones; on NX the application starts, goes to the background and its log has no fatal line. **Not seen:** a notification from an extension shown and answered from outside the window.
- **Note.** While this was written, another writer changed `bridge.rs` twice (it duplicated the new code and broke `language`); what it left was removed and the file compiled again before the build.

### E-097 — Key bindings, steps 2–4: the registry, the command, the Binds page and the window's chords (desktop) — IN CODE; contract, core, engine and window suites pass (window 716), lint clean; the Binds page SEEN in the headless window (`shots/desktop_binds_page_headless.png`); not yet seen in a real desktop window

- **Contract.** `Action::bindable()` is the one rule (no `input`, not destructive) for a suggested chord and for a binding.
- **Core (`bindings.rs`).** `BindTarget` is one of the application's four actions (accept or decline the incoming files, show Send, show Receive) or an extension's action, with a device exactly when it needs one. `Settings.bindings` maps a chord to it: one chord one meaning, one chord per target (`bind` refuses a held chord with what holds it and moves a target's chord). Three tests.
- **Engine.** `set-bindings` replaces them all, after checking every extension action is installed, bindable and names a paired device exactly when it needs one; refused on Android. `update-settings` keeps what is bound; `patch-settings` has no field for it. Removing an extension and forgetting a device drop their bindings. The installation test of the engine now binds the test extension's action, sees two refusals, and sees the binding go with the extension: passes.
- **Window.** Settings has **Binds** after Permissions, on a desktop only (`SettingsTab::shown()`; its own keyboard picture). Each row is something a key can run (the application's four, then every bindable action of every installed extension, one row per paired device where it needs one), with its chord, Record (the next chord pressed; Escape stops), Clear, and "Use …" for a free suggested chord; a taken chord is refused with the words of what holds it. While the window is in front, a bound chord runs what its row says, the way its button runs it (`run_bound`); nothing runs while a chord is being recorded. `chords.rs` reads a key press as a chord once (letters by what the layout prints, digits and named keys by their place). 14 new words in the 11 languages.
- **Not done:** chords outside the window (the portal on Linux), and the proof with Control.

### E-098 — Key bindings, step 5: up to three chords per action, a description under each, and the desktop asked for them outside the window (Linux) — IN CODE; contract, core and window suites pass, lint clean; desktop window and the portal NOT yet seen

- **Asked by the owner (2026-11-16):** a binding is not one-shot: each action lists all its chords (up to three), each with Remove, and **+ New** adds one; chords may not collide; an extension's suggested chord gives way to what a person bound. Each row: its title (a glance), under it its description (exactly what it does, optional, bounded, scrolling inside a short box when long), then the bindings.
- **Core (`bindings.rs`).** `MAX_PER_TARGET = 3`; `bind` adds beside what a target has and is refused with `Refused::Taken(what holds it)` or `Refused::Full`; `chords_of`, `within_limits` (what the owner checks of a whole map). `AppAction::help()`. `Settings.bindings_outside` (off; `patch-settings` field). The engine refuses a map past the limit.
- **Contract (`peersus-api`).** `Action.help`: optional, one line, at most `MAX_HELP` (280) characters, refused otherwise (one test). Older applications refuse a manifest that has it, like any field they do not know.
- **Window.** `BindRow` has `chords`, `help`; `rebound` refuses with `BindRefusal::{Taken, Full}`; `unbound` takes one chord out. The Binds page: title, description (a long one scrolls inside 54 px), then the bindings themselves as buttons, with an add button inline after them (outlined, in the accent, `+`) while there is room: pressing it records a chord, which becomes a binding in its place with a new `+` after it; pressing a binding records a new chord in its place, Backspace or Delete removes it, Escape leaves it. While recording, the button shows the modifiers held so far (`ctrl+alt+…`, `chords::held_words`). No other list, row or button (the owner refused a separate "none / + New / Use …" row on 2026-11-16). A Linux-only switch "Also while the window is not in front".
- **Suggested chords are bound, not offered.** On a fresh install (not an update) the owner binds each suggested chord of an action that needs no device where the key is free (`bindings::bind_suggested`, one test): an extension's key gives way to whatever holds it. The contract and `docs/api/manifests.md` say so.
- **Outside the window (Linux, `outside_chords.rs`).** With the switch on and something bound, the window registers itself as a host application and asks the GlobalShortcuts portal (version 2 on the workstation's GNOME 51) for each chord (id = the chord's text, trigger in the shortcuts specification's words, description = the row's words), on a thread of its own; a new session for each change of the list, none while the switch is off or nothing is bound, so the desktop's dialog never opens for a person who did not ask. A press it reports runs what the chord is bound to; one that shows a page brings the window forward. A press that the window and the desktop both report runs once (`run_chord`, 300 ms).
- **Test extension.** Five bindable actions: three on a new method `bump` (one with a short description and the suggestion `ctrl+alt+k`, one with a long description, one with none; its page counts "Run by a key N times"), and two that ask for the page's two notifications, `notify` (in the window, `ctrl+alt+n`) and `question` (also outside it, `ctrl+alt+q`), counted as a press of their buttons. Guest tests pass (5); its manifest passes `check-manifest`. Installed on the workstation's development instance (MSI) and enabled; `invoke-action count-key` through the control socket: done.
- **Seen by the owner on the workstation (2026-11-16):** recording `alt+q` works. Not yet seen: the redesigned row, the portal's dialog and a press with the window behind others.

### E-099 — A layered picture sometimes drawn without one of its layers (the keyboard of Binds with no keys) — FIX IN CODE; cause INFERRED

- **Reported by the owner** with a picture: the Binds tab's keyboard drawn as a plain filled box with its outline and no keys, sometimes, and right other times; cause unknown to him.
- **Read.** A layered picture was drawn as up to four `SvgViewer`s stacked at the same place (shape, second shape, detail, outline), each rasterised and cached on its own (key: bytes, size, colour). The keys are the detail layer, in a colour that the colours of this theme make black on the accent, so not a colour that vanishes. Which of the viewer's states leaves one layer undrawn while the others show was not found by reading the toolkit's cache and was not reproduced: **inferred**, not shown.
- **Changed.** `glyph::draw_duo` puts the layers together into one SVG (each layer's content with its colour written in, inside the line's own tag, which every layer shares; a test holds that), drawn by one viewer and kept per picture and colours (bounded). A picture is now drawn whole or not at all, with one raster instead of up to four.
- **Not yet:** seen in the desktop window over time.

### E-100 — Languages: English in the code, every other language a PO file on the `languages` branch, a Languages page, the person's language for extensions — IN CODE; contract, core and owner suites pass; Android package built on the final language code; NOT yet seen on a device or fetched from the real branch

- **Asked by the owner (2026-11-16):** the built-in catalog becomes gettext files kept on their own branch (renamed by him from `language` to `languages`), fetched like the store, detected from the device, importable from a file, listed with how much each translates; extensions learn the person's language.
- **Added (`peersus-api`).** `language.rs`: `Language` (a validated tag, `Copy`), the PO parser and the Plural-Forms evaluator (bounded, never panics), `Catalog` with `problems`/`without_problems` (a translation whose placeholders differ from its English is dropped). `Contributions::translate`: labels and help of pages, actions and settings, never ids or choices, never into an invalid label.
- **Added (owner).** `languages.rs`: the offer (`languages/<release>/languages.json`) and the file of the wanted language fetched through the store's fetcher (`languages-tree`, then blobs), cached per release under the data directory, imports kept apart and preferred; offline stays English and tries again later. Commands `LanguageImport`, `LanguageText`, removal, check again. Service `host` / `peersus.language` / `1`: `get` and the event `changed`, no permission. `package_languages.rs`: a package's own `languages/<tag>.po` (at most 64, plain files, header and name agree) translate its manifest words in the WINDOW's list only; `peersus.plugins` still tells the manifest as written.
- **Added (window).** `crates/peersus-ui/languages/messages.pot` (the template); `i18n.rs` reads the shown file; Settings → Languages (follow the device, each language with its share translated, use, remove, import, save the template, share); the quick ribbon lists the same choices. Android: the system file picker hands the chosen file's descriptor to Rust, which reads it bounded and imports it; its messages are said in the window's language (read from the owner's state, never from a window context, and inside the panic guard).
- **Added (every sentence a person can be shown).** The owner's sentences (refusals, errors, reasons: 276 of them) were not in the template, so they stayed English in every language; the window's own screens had literals drawn untranslated (the screen of an owner that would not start, a transfer's state words, presence words, the header's state, folder messages, file-dialog titles, the private catalog setup). All are in the template now and drawn through the translator. `i18n/coverage.rs` replaces the old scan of a hand list of files: a small lexer reads every source file of the window (any literal that reads as words is a message unless it is a name) and the owner's files that hand the window a sentence (`Err(`, `Refusal(`, `.error(`, `ok_or(`, `map_err(|_| …)`, match arms, named constants), skipping comments, logging and formatting macros and tests.
- **Changed (Settings search).** Each page gives search its own entries through `settings_search::Index` (`add` a thing it shows, `push` a record with its own anchor); `entries` asks every tab through one exhaustive match, so a new page cannot be left out. Languages and Binds are searchable (each language as its row says, the page's buttons; each binding by what it does, its words, extension, device and keys). `view::SETTING_ENTRIES` is gone (its rows moved to their pages). Matching folds marks (`view::folded`): "setari" finds "Setări", "jezyk" "Język".
- **Added (tools and CI).** `cargo xtask languages-offer` / `check-languages`; `.github/workflows/languages-check.yml` (on `main`; its copy goes to the branch once `main` carries the template). Twelve languages for 0.1.0 in the git-ignored local copy `languages/0.1.0/` (the eleven and Chinese `zh`, asked by the owner for an end-to-end test), each translating all 988 messages.
- **Removed.** The compiled-in catalog of the window, and five messages of the clipboard extension that it carried although no code of the application shows them (an extension's words are its own, now in its package).
- **MSI.** The dev binary had been replaced by a test build with the in-memory identity (a test run with `test-identity` used the same target directory), so the window said "Identity unavailable"; the keyring's two records were untouched. Rebuilt with `./dev.sh desktop`; the owner started with its extensions. Test builds with `test-identity` use `target/ui/test-identity` again. The eleven files were put in this device's release cache by hand, and the owner listed them after a restart.
- **Not yet:** the `languages` branch is committed locally (`target/languages-branch`) but its push was refused by the repository's guard: the repository's own `user.signingkey` is not the owner's allowed key, and overriding it is blocked; waiting on the owner. No live fetch made; nothing seen on NX or ROG; the translations other than Romanian are machine-written and not reviewed by speakers.

### E-101 — Nothing reaches an extension without a permission the person gave, per part and per device; Monitor rebuilt on it; languages say when a machine translated them — IN CODE; contract, core, owner and window suites pass, Monitor and Control suites pass; NOT yet seen in a window or on a phone

- **Asked by the owner (2026-11-16):** the security root: nothing is exposed through the API without the person's permission, each scope apart. A watcher of its own for it (PAZNIC, one scope, instructions in Romanian).
- **Changed (owner).** Every method and event the application offers an extension is guarded, except the caller's own (`CALLER_OWN_METHODS`: its settings, its pages, its actions, its statuses, its licence answer); a test fails for anything else offered unguarded, one fails for a permission not decided per device or once, and one fails for a guard the plugin that imports it could not be granted. New guards: `peers.watch` (per device: `peers.list`, the devices in `peersus.config`, and `status.list` show only devices it may see), `plugins.watch`, `platform.read`, `language.read`, `displays.watch`, and `peersus.system` split into six methods, each with its own permission (`device.info.cpu/gpu/ram/storage/network/battery`). `status.changed` carries `{}`: one payload goes to every listener, so it cannot name a device one of them may not see.
- **Fixed (found by review before release).** What a plugin could be granted was a second hand list beside the table of what the application offers, and it lacked every new guard: the person could not have granted any of them, and each would have answered `denied` for ever. It is now read from that one table (`host_exports`) for exactly what the plugin imports.
- **Changed (contract).** `Node::Drawing` (outlines in a bounded subset of SVG path notation, in theme colours) and `Style.scroll`/`wrap`, in place of the gauge and graph elements.
- **Changed (Monitor, local copy `plugins/peersus-monitor`).** Its page sits in its settings; the setting "Add Monitor to page" also places it among the pages (`peersus.pages`). Each part is asked on its own; a part not allowed says "Not allowed" in its card and the rest show. Cards with their own pictures; the battery drawn filled from the bottom with its minutes; a grid with as many columns as the width holds, the battery tall at the right on a wide page; the bar shows the devices it may see, online or asleep. Control opens with no device instead of failing when it may see none.
- **Changed (languages).** A PO header `X-Translation: ai` (or `person`) travels with the file into the offer and the held list (`LanguageFile.machine`); the row says "AI translated". It is not permanent: a review of the whole file changes `ai` to `person`. The eleven machine-written files say `ai`, Romanian does not. Two messages and five desktop-arrangement refusals added to the template and all twelve files.
- **Not yet:** the Monitor and the permission pages seen in a window and on a phone; the `languages` branch synced and pushed.

### E-102 — A demo that shows one extension as it really runs; the gate on translated sentences had a hole, 30 sentences closed through it — IN CODE; window suite 723 pass, language files check; the demo window running on the workstation, NOT yet looked at by the owner

- **Changed (window, `demo` feature).** `--demo-extension=DIR` (`demo_owner.rs`, `link.rs` `Session::extension_preview`): two owners of the demo's own in the window's process, paired, in-memory identities, loopback only, a temporary folder; the unpacked package is staged as a package the person chose, and its licences, notice, warning and permissions are the person's to accept in the window. Nothing is accepted or allowed by the demo. The link tests' test owners are the same code. `docs/development.md` says how to run it, with the layout that puts the helpers beside the window.
- **Fixed (gate).** `i18n/coverage.rs` skipped every sentence beginning with a one-letter word ("A plugin…") and did not read `Failed(` as a sentence handed on, so 30 sentences the window shows were in no language: among them "A plugin stopped working and was turned off" (the fixed message of the isolation rule) and "A plugin could not complete the action: what it needs is not available" (the one the owner saw with the test plugin). Both holes closed; the files that define the permissions' words (`platform_services.rs`, `transfer_events.rs`) are read; the 30 added to the template and to the twelve files. They were written by the agent in every language, Romanian included, whose file says `person`: its 30 new lines are not reviewed by a speaker. Two refusals of an input that cannot be staged say the one translated sentence they mean. `status_service.rs` stays unread: its refusals answer the extension, never the person.
- **Fixed (the owner's "test is broken", 2026-11-16).** The toast "A plugin could not complete the action: what it needs is not available" comes, read in the code, from the hub's answer to a call whose provider ended before answering, which is what the test extension's misbehaving buttons do on purpose (leave, spin, fill its memory); which button the owner pressed is not known. The hub said `unavailable`, the code of a provider that is not there. It now says `stopped` (`hub.rs` `disconnect`), read as `ActionFailure::Stopped`: "A plugin stopped before it finished the action" on the toast, "The plugin stopped before it finished. Retry when it runs again." on the action's row; both in the template and the twelve files. Contract table in `docs/api/service-messages.md`. Not yet seen in a window.
- **Fixed (lint).** A needless borrow in an owner test.

### E-103 — The three largest files split along what they already did; one cycle of modules gone — IN CODE; owner suite 725 pass, window suite 718 pass, workspace lint clean; behaviour unchanged by intent, NOT seen in a window after the split

- **Changed (owner).** `runtime.rs` (7 972 lines) keeps the loop (`run`, `execute`) and the start of the runtime; what it offers extensions and the rules on who may use it are `runtime/host_contract.rs`, `permissions.rs`, `outcomes.rs`, `admission.rs`; the rest of the loop by concern is `runtime/packages.rs`, `transfers.rs`, `settings.rs`, `plugin_calls.rs`, `publishing.rs`; its tests are `runtime/tests.rs`. Moved code is unchanged; what one part calls of another is `pub(super)`, nothing wider.
- **Changed (window).** `lib.rs` (10 478 lines) gave each page, by its own section markers, a module: `send_page.rs`, `receive_page.rs`, `transfers_page.rs`, `devices_page.rs`, `plugin_manager.rs`, `settings_page.rs`; the root, the frame and the shared pieces stay. `view.rs` (13 412 lines) keeps its rules; its main test module is `view/tests.rs`, and the send circle's rules with their tests are `view/circle.rs`.
- **Fixed (Android build).** The system readings read a volume's space through `fs2`, which the owner listed for desktops only, so the Android package no longer built; `fs2` is now for every platform. `serde` and `rand`, named by the Android crate and used by none of its code, are gone. `./dev.sh android --container peersus-ui-build` builds the package again (on the host, Skia's bindings find no C++ headers).
- **Fixed (cycle).** Settings search and the five pages that give it entries depended on each other; the entry, its target and the index are `settings_index.rs`, which the pages use, so `scripts/module-cycles.py` finds no group in any crate.
- **Fixed (gate).** The check that every sentence the owner can show is in the template read the owner's files by a flat list, so the sentences that moved under `runtime/` would have gone unchecked; the moved files are on the list, and the check passes with them.
- **DRY pass, looked at and decided.** (1) Errors into words: each surface has one path (`runtime/outcomes.rs` for what the owner says of a plugin's call, `view::action_words` for an action's own line); the hub's wire codes (`Gate::reject`) and the diagnostic codes (`peersus_diagnostics::refusal`) read the same `peersus_api::Error` on purpose with different vocabularies (a coarse public contract, an exact log word), so they stay apart. (2) The release of what a silent controller holds was already one path (`release` and `let_go`, E-054). (3) Control's guest closed a stream whose write failed in two places, one without a diagnostic; both are now `Remote::abandon` (Control suite 65 pass). The delivery check that Clipboard and Control both make (half-close, read the count, compare) differs in Control by waiting on the control channel meanwhile, so it was not moved into the shared guest glue.
- **Dead code, by a count and not by the lint alone.** The lint sees only what Linux compiles, so every function, constant and type the workspace and the extensions' working copies name was counted across all of them, with test modules and test files apart. Removed, named by nothing: `PluginManager::owner_is_running`, `NetworkHost::is_asleep`, `view::FUSE_SPARKS`, `view::HEADER_TAB_ROUND`, the English-only wrappers `seen_words`, `offer_words`, `chosen_words`, `chosen_words_total` and `history_line` (the tests have their own three). Removed with their tests, named only by tests, rules whose screen is gone: `View::has_finished_transfers`, `View::window_title` (the window is titled "Peersus", the header names the device), `CodeCircle::lets_choose`, `going_on`, `confirm_ms`, `StoreAction::can_remove`, `store_rows` and `store_empty` (the store's search and empty list are `extension_rows` on the Extensions page now), `Form::profile_shows_name`, `Waiting::lets_the_circle_choose` and `offers_more_and_clear`, the whole `scatter` module (the pairing numbers are in a row, rotated, not scattered), `layout::snap`, `Package::is_newer_than`, `catalog::is_update`, `Catalog::plural_rule`. Kept for tests only, now `#[cfg(test)]`: `Invitations::create_for_files`, `Pictures::own_digest`, `qr::Drawn::drawings_made` and `is_for`, `Store::with_base`. Kept: the probes the integration tests of `peersus-net` read, the headless test window, and `transport::connected_pair`, which the extensions' tests use (removed once by mistake and put back). Dependencies used only by tests moved to `[dev-dependencies]`: `rand` in the window, `peersus-api` in the plugin runtime.
- **Extensions.** `scripts/module-cycles.py` takes crate folders now and was run on the working copies of every extension: Control's guest had a group of three (`layout` ↔ `remote` ↔ `ui`, the state of all three kept in `remote`); the state is the crate root's (`State` in `lib.rs`), so the pages need the remote part one way only. Control suite 65 pass, lint clean for the host, `wasm32` and the `native` feature. Left for the rebuild of the extensions: Clipboard's native crate decodes pictures (`picture::decode_png`) that nothing of it receives.
- **Languages.** The working copy of the `languages` branch had lost `X-Translation: ai` in the eleven machine-written files (its offer called all twelve written by a person), and the two copies held different messages: the branch copy the 30 sentences of E-102, the default `languages/0.1.0` the 18 of E-101 (the permission words of each part, "AI translated", the five desktop-arrangement refusals and Monitor's description). The header is back in the branch copy; copying it over the default folder first lost those 18, which were then taken back word for word from the edits that had written them (the Romanian one in its corrected voice) and added to the branch copy, never by overwriting a whole file. The offer now counts 1041 of 1041 messages in all twelve files, `machine` for eleven and not for Romanian; the default folder is the same files; `check-languages` passes on both.
- **Each program said apart (the owner's ask of 2026-11-15: "clear what is UI, what is CORE, what is plugin").** What a Linux monitor shows as a program's publisher is read from the package it came from (an RPM's vendor, a Flatpak's metadata, as the owner's own monitor does), never from the program file, so on Linux the place for it is the application's AppStream file: it now lists all five programs it installs (`peersus-ui`, `peersus`, `peersus-wasm-host`, `peersus-native-host`, `peersus-store-fetch`, before only the first two) and says in its description what each one does (validated, no new finding). Windows and macOS keep this in each program (a version resource per `.exe`, an `Info.plist` per helper) and a monitor reads it there; those come with the packages of those systems and are a task of the platforms phase, not written ahead of them.
- **Fixed (window's language tests).** `crates/peersus-ui/tests/language.rs` read sentences the trimmed test language files (`src/i18n/fixtures/`) no longer held, and four of its five tests failed; the eleven messages they read were added to the Romanian, German and Polish fixtures, word for word from the language files. Whole workspace afterwards: 2236 tests pass, none fail (`--no-fail-fast`).

### E-104 — A package installed from the command line starts with nothing its name was allowed; every way of updating says what happens to the grants — IN CODE; tests pass; not run on an installed build

- **The gap (E-061).** Consent belongs to a plugin's name. The window and the store forget what a name was granted in the same step as they remove or replace its package; `plugin-remove` only took the files away and left the forgetting to the next start, which forgets only names no longer installed. So a package removed and another installed under the same name from the command line, both before the application started again, inherited the first one's grants.
- **Changed.** `plugin-install` (`Manager::install_accepted`, the command line's only way in) puts a mark, `new-install`, into the copy before it is moved into place, so the package is never at its name without it; it is one of the files the application writes into a plugin's folder (`is_the_applications_own`), so it changes neither what was approved nor what is measured. At start, right after `forget_removed` and before anything is registered, `forget_new_installs` forgets what each marked name was granted, on every device and outright, and takes the mark away; a mark that cannot be taken away stops the start as a grant that cannot be forgotten does, because left it would forget at the next start what the person allows in this one. What the running application installs (window, store, automatic update) is not marked: it forgets the old grants itself.
- **Said where it happens.** `plugin-install`: "It has no permissions yet, whatever an earlier package of that name was allowed". `plugin-remove`: what it was allowed is forgotten when the application starts again. The help of `store-install`, `plugin-install` and `plugin-remove` says the same; the window's update button already said that an update forgets what the old version was allowed, but only on an opened card and not for the "Automatic updates" switch, which updates without a press: the same sentence (already in every language file) now stands under the switch's name. `docs/development.md` describes the rule, and that `scripts/deploy-plugin.py`, through the running application, is the way to replace one's own development build and keep its grants.
- **And while the application runs.** A running application registers a plugin once (at start, or when it installs or approves one) and its supervisor starts the program again after a crash or a restart by the program's path: removed and installed again from the command line meanwhile, or changed by hand, the new program would have been started with the manifest and the grants of the one it replaced. Every launch of a plugin installed in the store now goes through `plugin_folder::Registered`, which starts it only while its folder is ready and carries no `new-install` mark; otherwise the launch fails like any other and the supervisor gives up after its usual tries, and the next start sees the folder for what it is. The process launcher the supervisor used to make for itself is given apart for this (`Service::process_launcher`); the module launchers are wrapped the same way.
- **Tests.** Three new: a package installed from the command line is marked and ready, the start forgets its name's grants and no one else's, takes the mark away, and a second start keeps what was allowed meanwhile; a package the running application installs is not marked; a registered plugin is launched while its folder is as registered, and not once the mark is there or its program was changed.

### E-105 — The synthetic measurements against the run kept before the refactor — MEASURED; no regression found in what they cover; one apparent one explained

- **How.** `cargo xtask bench --no-build` (programs built in release in the build container from this tree, run on the workstation, the window on a private Xvfb), kept as `benchmark-results/after-refactor-20261116*.json` beside `before-refactor-20261114.json`. A first run had a load of 15 to 20 (the two test guests, browsers, the owner's monitor): every number moved, pure serialization included (+12 %), so it was not used. The guests were paused (`virsh suspend`, resumed afterwards) and two runs taken back to back at a load of about 7.5: they agree within a few percent with each other (the "quiet" columns below). The baseline's load was not recorded and it was taken from a tree with changes not committed, so it cannot be rebuilt: the comparison is indicative, on one machine.
- **Same or better.** Opening and drawing a screen with no display (`replay`): Settings 162 → 129 ms, Transfers 168 → 133–136 ms, Send 182 → 172 ms. First frame of the real window: 998 → 215–222 ms on Send, with the work before it 652 → 138 ms and 18 MB less memory at start. Encrypted loopback 137 → 134 MiB/s, state encoding 41 → 45 µs and decoding 128 → 130–135 µs (within the spread). Settled rate of every idle window 0.2 frames a second, as before: the Transfers page no longer redraws while nothing changes (the 8–9 a second of the todo is not there in this measurement).
- **Apparent regression, explained.** The quiet window (Send, nothing moving) drew 1.1 frames a second over its ten measured seconds before and about 10 now, at 20 % of a core instead of 2 %. A 20-second run draws the same ~100 frames as a 4-second one, all in the first two seconds, and pictures of the private display taken meanwhile show what moves: the header writing out the name (E-041) and the scroll bar fading. Both are timed from creation; with the first frame a second earlier than before, they now run inside the measured window instead of before it. The window that replays 30 changes a second draws 137 frames a second instead of 164, at 276 % of a core instead of 332 %: fewer frames for the same changes, not slower frames.
- **Not measured here:** the input path between devices (capture, forwarding, replay), which needs the devices (E-019, E-023); frame times on the phones; idle wake-ups of a running owner with devices and extensions.

### E-106 — Extension pages in one place, behind a setting; the demo shows several extensions; two faults found by running it — IN CODE; window and core suites pass; SEEN in the demo window on a private display (Layout and Monitor in one place); NOT on a phone

- **Asked by the owner (2026-11-16):** an option that puts the extensions' pages in one tab, "Extensions", as in the concept of the site (a phone whose Extensions tab switches Remote | Monitor); gated by a setting.
- **Changed (core).** `Settings.extension_pages_together` (off by default, absent from the file while off), and its field in `SettingsPatch`.
- **Changed (window).** With the setting on, every `primary` page of an extension is reached through one place of the main navigation, `Page::Extensions` ("EXTENSIONS", the extensions picture); `contributed_page::Shared` draws a row of the pages' names and pictures (shown from two pages on, the chosen one filled) over the chosen page, and the choice is kept by the connected window while the person goes elsewhere. Off, each page has its own place as before. A page that lost its own place when the person put them together is found in the shared place, and a place with nothing left falls to Send. The setting is a switch on Appearance, "Extension pages in one place", in Settings search, in all twelve language files (1044 of 1044). `docs/api/service-messages.md` says that where `primary` pages appear is the window's choice and that an extension learns nothing of it.
- **Changed (demo).** `--demo-extension` may be given up to eight times; each package is staged in the demo's owner. `--demo-page=extensions` opens the shared place.
- **Fixed (found by running it).** (1) The demo's owners were given the folder `plugins/` as their data directory, so the store was `plugins/plugins` and an acceptance had no folder to be written to: every install in the demo ended with "Your acceptance could not be recorded, so nothing was installed". They are given their data directory now, as the application's owner is. (2) An extension's page drawn where another had been drawn (the switcher) kept the first page's state and never asked for its own content, showing "Loading…" for ever: `contributed_page::Elements` is now one instance per extension and page (`render_key`), so it starts afresh and asks.
- **Seen** (the demo built from this tree, on a private X display, driven with the pointer): Control and Monitor installed through the licence, notice and outside-store warning; their "Add … to page" switches on; with the setting off, two places of their own (Layout, Monitor) in the header; with "Extension pages in one place" switched on in Appearance, one EXTENSIONS place with the row Layout | Monitor, the chosen one filled and its picture in the filled ink; Monitor chosen draws Monitor's page (every part "Not allowed", nothing having been allowed). Layout stays "Loading…" in the demo, in its own place as in the shared one: the demo's owners have no display service. Seen in the same picture and handed to Monitor's work: the title rows of Monitor's cards are misplaced (CPU's missing, others pushed right, Storage cut at the left).
- **Not done:** a phone (Remote is Android's), the owner's look.

### E-107 — Clipboard says why a share failed — IN CODE (local copy `plugins/`, for its submission); guest tests pass (76); NOT seen on a device

- **Why.** The owner sometimes sees only "A plugin could not complete the action". The clipboard guest answered every failure with its own code `failed`, which the application can only turn into that sentence, even when the application had told it why (a device it may not send to, a service not available, a busy or stopped peer).
- **Changed.** The guest keeps the first of the application's own codes (`denied`, `refused`, `unavailable`, `stopped`, `timeout`, `busy`) given for a call of its that failed, and answers with it; any other code is not repeated, and without one it still says `failed`. One test (four codes, one made up).
- **Also changed (owner).** A platform call of an extension that the owner finds not allowed (permission withdrawn after the broker let the call through) was answered `unavailable`, "not available" to the person; it is `denied` now, and a full queue of platform calls is `busy`; only a platform that offers no such facility stays `unavailable` (`runtime/plugin_calls.rs`). The broker itself already refuses a call not allowed with `denied`, so this is the race only. Owner suite: clippy 0, 727 pass.
- **Window.** The names in the row of the shared Extensions place are cut with an ellipsis at 240 px (a page's name may be 80 characters). `demo_owner::stage` is compiled only with the demo, which the window's tests without it warned of.
- **Not found:** the cause of the rare clipboard failure itself. The "what it needs is not available" the owner saw with the test extension has a known producer besides the platform branch fixed here: the broker refuses an action call to an extension that is not registered, not connected or suspended (`hub.rs`, `Error::Unavailable`, answered as `unavailable`), and the test extension's flood suspends it (E-030). To be traced on the next occurrence from the extension's status at the press and the `broker-route`/`action-failed` trace. The process variant (`src/plugin.rs`) is unchanged. The new sentence of the owner is in `messages.pot` and the twelve language files (1045 of 1045, offer regenerated and checked in both copies).

### E-108 — Vendored copies against their upstreams — CHECKED (crates.io, 2026-11-16); none can be dropped yet; each is on the newest upstream its consumers accept

- **Read:** each `vendor/*/PATCHES.md` (what it changes and when it goes) and crates.io's newest releases and their dependencies.
- **Current with upstream:** `accesskit_unix` 0.24.1, `accesskit_atspi_common` 0.21.1, `freya-core`/`-components`/`-winit` 0.6.0-alpha.1, `freya-skia-bindings` 0.105.0-beta.1, `mundy` 0.2.3, `pulp` 0.22.3, `rav1e` 0.8.1, `winit` 0.30.13 (0.31 is only in beta).
- **Newer upstream exists, but the patch must stay:**
  - `atspi` 0.30 / `atspi-common`, `-proxies` 0.14 and `zbus-lockstep` 0.7 are still on zbus 5; the copies are here to move them to zbus 6 (no unmaintained wire packages). `accesskit_unix` 0.24.1 itself asks for `atspi` 0.29, so 0.29 is the release to carry. Goes when an upstream on zbus 6 is accepted by accesskit.
  - `netwatch` 0.20: the newest `iroh` (1.4.1, the one in use) and `portmapper` still ask for 0.19.3; the copy backports 0.20's netlink set only. Goes when iroh moves.
  - `sctk-adwaita` 0.12 needs a newer windowing stack than `winit` 0.30; the copy backports its font renderer only. Goes with winit 0.31.
  - `keyring` 4.2 is a new design (a separate core and stores); moving to it is a change of the credential backend, with installed identities to keep, not a patch removal. Not done; the copy only removes unused features.
- **Nothing changed in the tree.**

### E-109 — Build speed: debug information trimmed, the build folder on the NVMe, the build without the container, the job cap removed — MEASURED on the workstation (8 cores, 46 GB, Rust 1.96.1)

- **Asked by the owner (2026-11-16):** faster incremental builds, the build cache on the NVMe, what else helps (looked up), and whether the build needs the Distrobox container.
- **Debug information (Cargo book, "Optimizing Build Performance").** `[profile.dev] debug = "line-tables-only"` and `[profile.dev.package."*"] debug = false`: this project's code keeps file and line numbers for backtraces, libraries keep none. On the old disk (HDD), one change in the window: 18 s instead of 47-103 s; the build folder 4.4 GB instead of 8.4 GB. A debugger session that needs variables sets `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=true` for that build.
- **Build folder on the NVMe.** `target/ui` is now a link to a folder in the user's cache on the NVMe (btrfs, copy-on-write off for it). The old folder on the HDD is kept as `target/ui.hdd` until the owner says it may go; so are the two experiment folders `target/ui-host` and `target/ui-host-lt`. Measured with `cargo build --offline -p peersus-ui --bin peersus-ui --features demo` (the layout's environment, Skia built from source): from nothing 611 s (786 s on the HDD); after touching `crates/peersus-ui/src/view.rs` 7.8 s, 3.3 s and then 2.8-3.0 s in three more runs; after touching `crates/peersus-core/src/lib.rs` 8 s. Folder 4.8 GB.
- **Without the container: yes.** The same build runs on the workstation itself (Fedora 45, its own C/C++ and graphics headers); Skia is still built from source with FreeType embedded, as the policy asks. The container stays the way for an immutable host that lacks those headers (`./dev.sh desktop --container NAME`).
- **Job cap removed (a decision, not a measured gain).** `scripts/dev.py` set `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=2` for every `./dev.sh` build, with no recorded reason; on this workstation that used a quarter of its cores for every build with more than two crates to compile (the measurements above ran Cargo directly, with every core). Removed: Cargo uses every core by default, and `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS` set by the caller still applies, for a machine short of memory.
- **The Android build folder too.** `target/android` (5.6 GB, Rust libraries and Gradle's caches) was copied to the NVMe the same way and linked in its place; the HDD copy is kept as `target/android.hdd`. Not yet built there.
- **Tried and not kept.** `split-debuginfo = "unpacked"`: three incremental runs 2.8-3.0 s, the same as without it, and changing it rebuilds everything; not adopted.
- **Looked up and not adopted.** The linker: Rust 1.96 already links with its bundled `rust-lld` on this target; `mold` is not installed, and with the link already part of a 3 s rebuild there is little left to win. Cranelift is a nightly-only code generator and the project builds on stable 1.96. `sccache` helps clean builds and switching branches, not an incremental rebuild.

### E-110 — Monitor drawn as in the owner's concept; the readings it needs; a sibling-height length that did nothing until now; extension pages under a phone's header — IN CODE; guest tests pass (55); SEEN in the demo window on a private display, wide and phone-narrow (this device's real numbers); NOT on a phone or between two devices

- **Asked by the owner (2026-11-16):** the Monitor page as in `site/concept/` (two pictures: a wide desktop row and a phone column).
- **Readings (application, `system_readings`).** The processor also says its clock now (`mhz`, average of the cores), the graphics its clock and its own memory used and total (`mhz`, `memory_used`, `memory_total`), the storage the system's name of the disk received files are on (`device`, read from the mount table, absent where there is none, e.g. a folder in memory). The permission sentences of the three parts and `docs/api/service-messages.md` say so; the eleven languages carry the new sentences.
- **Contract.** `Tone::Figure` (a large bold number) for the figure in a ring or a battery. `Text.lines` (1 to 20): at most that many lines, the rest ending in an ellipsis; absent, as many as needed, and not written back, so a tree without it is unchanged. `Length::FillMinimum` was in the contract but no window drew it: every box laid its children out as `flex`, where the toolkit ignores it. A box that does not wrap and has a child with that length across it is now laid out to `fit` (the toolkit's condition), and the contract says that a `Flex` sibling is then not shared out.
- **Window: an extension's page started under the header's two pieces on a phone.** The application's own pages keep the room of those pieces free at their top; an extension's page (one place for all, or its own) did not, and its first row lay under the mark and the Settings button. Both then started under them. **Replaced by E-111:** an extension's page is now drawn inside the application's page frame, as Send is, and that wrapper is gone.
- **Compatibility, said plainly.** Page trees are read strictly (unknown fields and variants refused): an application from before this entry refuses a page that uses `Tone::Figure` or `Text.lines`, with its fixed message, and the extension keeps running. Monitor uses both, so it needs this application or a later one; nothing in the manifest says so yet (the contract has no version of the page elements apart from the manifest's own), which matters once the store offers it to installed applications of different ages.
- **Monitor.** Cards: processor and graphics (picture in a ring, name and model, busy share in a rounded tile, the last minute as a graph, clock and cores or memory used in two framed tiles, temperature over a bar coloured by warmth); memory (a three-quarter ring with the share large, sizes under it, as large as its card allows up to 150); storage (a ring beside its name and sizes, the disk's name in a band under it when known); network (graph, download and upload now, totals); battery (name in a band, the card filled from the bottom as far as it is charged, time left, charge, charging or not). Wide page: battery tall at the right; processor and graphics; then storage (38 %), network, memory (22 %); every card of a row as tall as the tallest. Narrow page: a grid of as many columns as fit. Sizes in binary units (`GiB`), small ones in bytes. Only the fields of each part in the table above are taken from another device.
- **Found while running it.** The layout subtracted the page's padding from a measure that is already inside it: 24 pixels were left at the right of a wide page (hidden until now by a flexible column). Fixed, tests moved to measured widths.
- **Narrow page (phone form, 420 wide).** Two columns as in the concept's phone picture, compact (smaller picture, number tile and tiles; the model on one line; storage's name over its ring like memory's), rows of equal height; the page starts under the header's pieces.
- **Seen.** Demo window (`--demo`, the packaged extension installed from file through the out-of-store warning, the six parts allowed one by one, "Add Monitor to page"), 1100 wide and then 420 wide: this desktop's processor, graphics, memory, storage and network shown live with their graphs; battery "Not available"; no guest trap or restart in the log.
- **Not verified.** A phone, a paired device's numbers, a battery that is present, a disk name on screen (the demo's received folder is in memory). An earlier build of this rework once ended in the interpreter's work bound (`OutOfFuel`) when the page opened; it was not seen again in seven runs of this one, and its cause was not found.

### E-111 — Extension pages inside the application's page frame; one chooser beside the mark; overlays clipped to the page; Settings fixes; transfer bar and button looks — DEPLOYED on NX and ROG (debug package, build number 4); SEEN in screenshots on NX

- **Asked by the owner (2026-11-16):** extension pages framed like Send, Receive, Peers and Transfers; in the one-place mode, the page chooser beside the application's mark.
- **Changed (window).** An extension's page is drawn inside the same page frame as the application's pages (the header pieces hide with it as on Send); in the one-place mode the chooser of pages sits in the header beside the mark; an extension's overlay box stays clipped to its page; Settings: a search result jumps to its row, the track of group pictures follows only the tabs shown; the transfer bar takes its colour from the transfer's state; solid buttons are drawn with a surface, a border and a dot; Pair and Connect are two halves of one control.
- **Seen.** On NX, screenshots after the package was installed on both phones (byte for byte, nothing uninstalled or cleared).

### E-112 — A device says what it takes from another, and an extension offers only devices where it is there and allowed — IN CODE; net, owner and contract suites pass; NOT yet seen between two devices

- **Asked by the owner (2026-11-16):** a device is shown as ready for an extension only where the other device has that extension and allows this device.
- **Changed (network).** A new stream request, `accepts` (at most 64 services, each checked as a stream's service is), which a device sends on every connection and again whenever what it takes changes; the other side keeps it per session. Older devices never send it; an unknown request kind was already refused cleanly.
- **Changed (owner).** What a device takes from another is worked out by the rule that admits a stream (`admission::accepted_from`: the export's receiving method and the permission given to that device), over the extensions that run and are switched on only (found by review: a stopped or switched-off extension was first said to take its service). It is told at every publish, so a permission, an extension started or stopped, or a new connection changes it. `peersus.peers` `list` gives each device `accepts`: only the asking extension's own services, by name; absent when not known.
- **Docs.** `docs/api/service-messages.md`: the field, and one rule for offering a device (online, allowed, and `accepts` naming the service; absent on an older host: by online and allowed as before).

### E-113 — The device chooser is the application's: one page element, chosen by rules the extension gives; Control and Monitor use it; an overlay no longer shows a sibling through it — DEPLOYED (package build 4 on NX and ROG; Control and Monitor installed on NX, ROG and HP, permissions kept where there were any); contract, owner, window, Control (65) and Monitor (54) suites pass; overlay fix SEEN on ROG; the chooser SEEN on NX ("Claude", this device) and on ROG (NX chosen, its numbers shown); NOT yet on a desktop

- **Asked by the owner (2026-11-16):** one chooser of devices in the API, used by Control and Monitor; customizable: what can be chosen (allowed, online, offline, sleeping, a custom status or exactly one), what each row shows (dot, status), picture and name always. Control does not take a sleeping device, Monitor does. And: "Left click" showed over Control's open menu.
- **Fixed (window).** An overlay box was raised one layer above its parent, and the toolkit raises every element one layer above its own parent, so text a few boxes deep in a sibling (the pad's "Left click") was painted over the open menu. It is now raised above the deepest tree a page can hold (`OVERLAY_RAISE`, from `MAX_DEPTH`), still inside its page.
- **Added (contract).** `Node::Devices {id, chosen, pick, show, above, this_device}`: `pick` = `presence` (any of online, sleeping, offline; default online), `allowed` (default true), `accepts` (one of the extension's own services), `status` (`{plugin}` or `{plugin, id}`, as `peersus.status` names it); `show` = `dot`, `presence` (default on), `statuses` (default off). `Event::Choose {id, device}` (none: this device). The devices (`entries`) are filled in by the application: a tree that writes one is refused.
- **Changed (owner).** Fills every chooser when the page answers and at every publish from what it knows: only the devices the extension may see (the rule of `list`), why each refused one is refused, statuses only with `status.watch` (without it a chooser that picks by status chooses nothing), `accepts` only for the extension's own service (another's is refused on every device, so nothing is learnt of another extension's consent). The window is given the owner's key of each device (for its picture); a choice comes back as that key and is turned into the extension's alias, only for a device the chooser offers as shown.
- **Changed (window).** The chooser frame (dot, picture, name, ▾) and the application's own menu, shared with the chooser of pages: refused devices dimmed with ONLINE/SLEEPING/OFFLINE, NOT ALLOWED, NOT READY THERE, NO STATUS or STATUS NOT ALLOWED; "This device" first where offered. Six new sentences in the template.
- **Changed (Control, Monitor; local copies in `plugins/`, for their submissions).** Both lost their own lists. Control: online, allowed, `control.pointer` there; a device where Control is not there is unselected with "Control is not ready there"; the page has no padding of its own, so the pad is wider. Monitor: this device first; online or sleeping (a sleeping device still answers and stays chosen), allowed, `monitor.readings` there; unselected when offline or where Monitor is not ready.
- **Compatibility.** A page that uses the element needs this application or a later one; an older one refuses the page with its fixed message.

### E-114 — Monitor on a phone: processor temperature, graphics, battery and network read where Android lets an application read them; how busy the processors are through the shell provider — DEPLOYED on NX and ROG; owner suite passes; SEEN on NX (temperatures 32–41 °C processor, 31–32 °C graphics, "Adreno75…", network totals, battery 85 % discharging, 32 h 4 min); the share busy SEEN on ROG in a screenshot (16 %), absent on NX (no provider allowed there)

- **Asked by the owner (2026-11-16):** what Monitor cannot read on a phone; Stellar/Shizuku only for what has no other way (graphics, for example).
- **Read on NX and ROG** (`run-as` with the application's own identity): `/proc/stat`, `/proc/loadavg`, `/proc/net/dev`, `/sys/class/power_supply/*` and `/sys/class/drm` are closed; `/proc/meminfo`, the cores' clocks, `/sys/class/kgsl/kgsl-3d0/gpubusy` and `gpu_model` and 99 thermal zones are open.
- **Fixed (processor temperature).** The temperature took the first entry of `/sys/class/thermal` whose type began with `cpu`; on a phone that is a cooling device (`cpu-hotplug4`), which has no temperature, so none was ever shown. Only thermal zones are read now: a computer's package zone, else the hottest core's.
- **Changed (graphics).** Without a readable graphics card (or with one that says nothing), the `kgsl` driver: its model, how busy over its last window (0 when powered down), the hottest of its zones. Its clock and memory are closed and stay unknown.
- **Changed (battery, network).** The platform's own interfaces, through the Android adapter (`PlatformReadings`): `BatteryManager` (charge, charging, minutes from the charge left and the current, or the time to full it computed) and `TrafficStats`. Nothing to grant.
- **Changed (processor, community distribution).** `/proc/stat` is read by a shell UserService (`ReadingsUserService`, one fixed file, answers this application only) through a Stellar/Shizuku provider the person already allowed; never asked for. A worker of its own reads the line once a second while it is asked for, and the owner is handed the last line read (at most three seconds old), so a provider that hangs never holds up the owner (found by review: the first version called the provider from the owner). Without one the share busy stays unknown. The Play distribution has none. `BatteryManager` and `TrafficStats` are calls into the system itself and stay direct.
- **Seen (NX, screenshots while the owner used it).** The overlay fix also on ROG with the old Control: "Left click" no longer shows over its open list. The Monitor installed on ROG has no permission yet (a fresh install keeps none), so it shows nothing there until the person allows it.
- **Asked by the owner (later the same day):** the battery card looked odd, and the cards were forced: they must use the whole device and arrange themselves. Seen on ROG: one column of cards narrower than the page.
- **Found (window).** One page can be drawn in two places at once (in its settings and in its own place), and its extension keeps one measure per id: the place that said its size last won. On ROG the settings card said it last, then closed, and the page kept a width of 262 for a page 301 wide: one narrow column. **Fixed:** the window keeps every measured box drawn now, by page and id; when one goes, the last one still drawn says its size again (`MEASURED`, `forget_measure`; one test).
- **Changed (Monitor).** A card is its share of the row it is in (a percentage of what holds it), never a pixel width: a row fills the page whatever its real width; the measure only chooses how many columns and how wide the graphs are drawn, and a graph is clipped to its card. The battery is a card like the others: its title at the top, its figures at the bottom, and the whole card filled from its bottom as far as it is charged, instead of a grey band over a separate filled box.
- **Changed (readings).** A phone's processor name, which its `/proc/cpuinfo` does not give, from the platform (`Build.SOC_MANUFACTURER`, `SOC_MODEL`: `QTI SM8650` on both phones).
- **Seen.** ROG (screenshot): this device's processor busy 16 % through the shell provider, 51 °C, graphics 41 °C. NX shows no share busy: no provider allowed there.

### E-115 — Monitor tells its problems through the application's notifications — IN CODE and INSTALLED (NX, ROG, MSI, HP); Monitor suite 55 pass; the notification NOT yet seen on a screen

- **Asked by the owner (2026-11-16):** what goes wrong in Monitor is told through the application's notifications, not written into the page.
- **Changed (Monitor, local copy).** One problem at a time (a chosen device unselected and why, a chosen device that does not answer, paired devices not shown at all) is shown once through `peersus.notify` `show` (imported; `notify.show` is the person's to grant), and again only if it goes and comes back. Without that permission (`denied`) the page writes it instead, from then on, so the person still learns it. "Asking …" stays on the page: it is how things stand, not a problem.

### E-116 — The window grew by over a megabyte a second while an extension's page drew a moving graph: each picture's raster was kept an hour — FIXED; a headless test holds it (fails without the fix: 31 rasters kept); measured before and after on the same machine, owner and extension; DEPLOYED to MSI and HP (desktop release)

- **Reported by the owner (2026-11-16)** with two pictures of the system monitor: the window at 784.6 MiB and 400.9 MiB of graphics memory, against 98.5 MiB and 81 MiB when fresh; Monitor's page suspected.
- **Measured on the owner's own process** (the desktop release of the morning, Monitor open, left running for it): anonymous memory grew 12.2 MB every 10 s, steadily (1.2 MB/s); after 50 minutes 1.73 GB of it was in swap besides 58 MB resident, and 404 MB of video memory was held. Files and shared memory did not move.
- **Cause (read, then proven).** An extension's `Node::Drawing` is drawn as SVG bytes made from its shapes; a graph that moves is new bytes each second. The toolkit's `SvgViewer` keeps each raster in its asset cache, keyed by the bytes, for `AssetAge::default()` = one hour after it stops being shown. So every second of a moving graph left a raster behind for an hour.
- **Changed.** `contributed_page::drawing_view` is the one place a drawing is shown; it asks the toolkit to drop a raster as soon as it is no longer shown (`AssetAge::zero()`). The application's own pictures (`glyph`) are fixed bytes and keep the default.
- **Test.** `headless::tests::a_drawing_an_extension_keeps_changing_keeps_no_raster_it_no_longer_shows` draws a graph 60 times through `drawing_view` on the toolkit's headless runner and reads the asset cache: at most 2 entries. With the line taken out, it fails with 31 entries (the 30 distinct pictures and the first); with it, it passes.
- **Measured, before and after** (this workstation, the same running owner and installed Monitor, a second window of the application on a private X display at 1280×800, Monitor's page open, resident and anonymous memory read every 10 s for 300 s). Before: a release of the current tree with the one line taken out (it also carries the later accent and coverage changes, which do not touch drawing): 164 MB of anonymous memory gained (70 → 235 MB, 0.55 MB/s, still climbing). After: the installed fixed release (built before those accent changes): 56.4–56.7 MB, resident 90 MB. The controlled comparison of the drawing path alone is the headless test above. The owner's window grew faster (1.2 MB/s) because it is larger and on a high-density screen: larger rasters.
- **Installed.** The fixed desktop release is on MSI and HP; the owner's window on MSI was restarted on it (the owner process kept running). Phones: in the next Android build.
- **Cost of a page that ticks, measured** (fixed release, same private display, 60 s each): Monitor's page 2.0 % of one core and 15 context switches a second in the window's process; the Send page 0.22 % and 5 a second. On a private X display nothing paces frames, so this is the most it costs. On Wayland a hidden window gets no frame callbacks and is not drawn while minimised; the extension is still asked once a second. Whether to stop asking while no window shows the page is the next decision; at these figures it is small, so it is left until a phone measurement says otherwise.

### E-117 — `peersus.displays` told every paired device's name to an extension holding only the one-for-the-computer `displays.watch` — FIXED: only the devices the person let that extension see; the places of the others are kept, never moved or named — IN CODE; display unit tests 6 pass; gate pending

- **Found by review (2026-11-16).** `display_service::inspect` listed every paired peer with its name and screens, and `arrange` resolved any of them, for a caller allowed `displays.watch`, which is decided once for the computer. The application's rule (E-101) is that a plugin learns a device exists only through `peers.watch` given for that device (`permissions::seen_by`).
- **Changed.** `inspect` and `arrange` take the desktops as one `Desktops` value and a `visible` test; the runtime passes `seen_by(peer, plugin)`. A device the extension may not see is not listed, not placed, and its alias names nothing to `arrange`; its saved place is kept as it is and counted in the overlap check. One helper (`displays_seen_by`) answers `inspect` and the reply after a save.
- **Tests (2 new):** a device not allowed is neither listed nor placed (its name, alias and place absent from the answer); arranging keeps the hidden device's place and refuses its alias.

### E-118 — Twelve integration tests failed after E-101 guarded `peersus.peers` `list` and `peersus.plugins` `list`: the tests were older than the rule, not the code wrong — tests brought to the contract; `plugins` suite 71 pass

- **Seen in the gate:** "result 1 denied" where a scenario expected a listing. Since E-101 the hub refuses `list` to a plugin no device allowed (`peers.watch`) and `plugins.list` without `plugins.watch`; the scenarios granted only `peers.send` or nothing.
- **Changed (tests only, nothing weakened).** Device grants that mean "list it and send to it" give `peers.watch` and `peers.send`; the hub-layer refusal of `send` is tested with `peers.watch` alone; the device-layer refusal with the other device seen but not allowed to be sent to. The test plugin keeps asking while a listing is refused (as while it is empty), and `ask-host` can wait for a gate file so a test allows a device first. New: a plugin no device allowed is refused even the list. The plugin-list scenarios grant `plugins.watch` outright (`grant_outright`), and an ungranted plugin's list is asserted refused.
