- **CI on `ef8ee51`** (dispatched by hand, as both workflows are): `Quality gates` green (Linux compilation and tests, the pinned Nix shell; run 44001292219); Linux artifacts, AppImage (type 2 runtime compared with the SHA-256 GitHub publishes for it, `617aa773…ed98a46`) and Flatpak succeeded. Android failed in a build script: `mundy` compiles Java and could not find `android.jar`. android-build looks for it under the platform named by `ANDROID_PLATFORM`; the runner has only `android-36`, and cargo-ndk 4 (the one CI installs; 3.5.4 here) names the minimum platform, 31 ([INFERENCE]: cargo-ndk 4's source was not read). `artifacts.yml` now checks that the compile SDK's `android.jar` is there and gives it as `ANDROID_JAR`; not yet run.
- **The input path, as E-019 measured it** (NX's Remote pad driving HP's own pointer; three strokes of eight seconds by `input swipe`, one way and back; HP's pointer device read with the kernel's event times; `/proc` ticks of 10 ms; both sides release builds now, E-019's controlled computer was a development build): 58.6 to 59.3 batches a second; gaps between batches 17.2–17.3 ms at the median, 20.1–21.5 for nine in ten, 32.2–36.2 for 99 in 100, never over 42.8 ms and no gap over 50 ms (E-019: 56–60 a second, 17.1, 20.4–20.7, 29–38, and 2 to 4 gaps of 80–130 ms in two strokes of three). Per stroke on HP: owner 24–31 ticks (4–6 at rest), Control's interpreter 12–14 (0), window 1–14; on NX: the application 296–306 (32–36 at rest), Control's worker 56–58 (E-019 after its fixes: 284–294 and 64–69). **Not measured:** the time from the touch to the pointer: the shell may not write to the touchscreen (SELinux refuses `sendevent`), and `cmd input` takes 32–71 ms, too wide a bracket for a latency of that order.
- **Frames on the phone** (SurfaceFlinger's present times of the window's layer; NX showed 60 Hz): flinging the Monitor page up and down, 1170 frames: 16.6 ms at the median and for nine in ten, 33.2 for 99 in 100, longest 83 ms, 2 % of intervals over one and a half frames; the window's thread at 22.5 % of a core over the run. At rest about one frame a second. Dragging the Remote pad draws few frames (nothing on the page changes). While the interface size is dragged every frame is laid out at a new size: about 30 frames a second.
- **A desktop at rest** (MSI, the release build of E-143 with its window open, five extensions, sixty seconds from `/proc`, as E-054 measured): owner 0.22 % of a core, 32.6 wake-ups a second, 37 MiB, 14 threads; window 0.20 %, 18.5 a second, 30 MiB; each of the five interpreters 0 %, none, 2–3 MiB. E-054 (the build of 17:30 on 2026-11-14): owner 0.50 %, 62 a second, 37 MiB; window 0.12 %, 18 a second, 98 MiB. A Windows guest was running tests on the same machine meanwhile; the wake-ups are the processes' own.
- **Found while measuring: a scroll that starts on a slider changes it (fixed in code).** Flicking Settings up and down with the finger landing on the interface size slider set the interface to 185 %, and the page did not scroll: the toolkit's slider puts the value where a press lands and stops the press, so the page never sees the touch. `vendor/freya-components/src/slider.rs` (`PATCHES.md`): a touch joins the window's one touch gesture of the scroll views as something that moves only along the slider; a drag along it moves it as a mouse does, a drag across it is the page's and leaves the value, a touch lifted where it landed is a tap that puts the value there. Mouse, keys and wheel unchanged. Tests `tests/slider_touch.rs` (three, the drag across fails on the old slider: the page stays at 0) and upstream's `tests/slider.rs`, both now run through `peersus-ui` like the scroll view tests: 30 toolkit tests pass. Those toolkit tests had never been part of the gate (its window tests are filtered to `demo` and `headless::`); `cargo xtask test` now runs `toolkit_` too, so CI guards the vendored changes. The gate on the tree with all of E-146: exit 0, 86 test results, 3194 passed (the 30 toolkit tests and the new size tests among them), 0 failed, 37 ignored, 153 script tests (a first run stopped on rustfmt and one Clippy lint in the new tests, both fixed). The phone's interface size was set back to 100 % through the control socket. **Seen on NX, build 20** (`PEERSUS_BUILD_NUMBER=20`, installed over build 19 on NX and ROG and verified byte for byte, data kept): a swipe up that starts on the slider scrolls Settings and the size stays 100 %; a drag along the slider changes it (180 %), set back to 100 % afterwards. ROG was locked and was not touched.
- **What 185 % did to a phone, and the two changes for it.** NX draws its screen at a density of 3.33 (its display made larger), so at 100 % the window is 335 logical pixels across, and at 185 % 181: far under the 300 every screen is laid out and checked at (`headless::tests::SIZES`). The header's Settings button lay over the page chooser, names were cut, and Remote's page broke "Right" inside the word and put the device chooser over the pad's captions. (1) **A window is never made narrower than 300 logical pixels by the interface size** (`interface_size::Percent::NARROWEST`, `largest`, `use_largest`): a saved size above what the window holds is drawn at the largest it holds, down to a step and never under 100 %, and at the saved one again when the window is wide enough; the slider's right end is that largest size, so the whole slider is live; the window's own size is now compared with what is saved (the whole scale also carries the screen's density). The width at 100 % is read when the laid-out width changes, with the size it was laid out at, since a new size changes the scale at once and the width one layout later. Tests: four new in `interface_size` (NX's 335 holds 110 %, every width keeps 300, an unknown width holds back nothing, the slider's end), the layout gate's narrowest width now that same constant; `peersus-ui` with its headless suite: 732 pass. **Seen on NX, build 21:** with 185 % saved the window drew at 110 %, the header whole, the slider at its right end showing 110 %; set back to 100 %. (2) **Control's captions on one line** (`lines: 1`, `control_caption`), on `submission/control-20261106` as `007b9f2`: at 185 % Remote showed "Ri…" and "Left" whole (seen on NX before the cap); Control's tests 69 pass, rustfmt clean; its source check passed again (44007837532) and its admission run was dispatched anew (44008922402) for the owner; the one for the older commit was cancelled. **Seen and not changed:** on Settings of a phone the track of group icons stands over the right edge of the page, also at 100 % on NX (the edge of `Remove` and of a long section title under it).

The cause is not known: `xtask android` now runs Gradle with `--stacktrace` (a run that passes prints nothing more) and the workflow prints the runner's free disk after a failed step, so the next run says what it is. **Cause, from that run (on `a0b62a2`, 44020536341):** `java.lang.OutOfMemoryError: Java heap space` in the packager (`zipflinger` reading a native library whole, `ApkFlinger.close`); the runner had 78 GB of disk free. Gradle's daemon had its default 512 MiB heap on both machines (no `org.gradle.jvmargs` anywhere); on this machine the same packaging fitted, on CI's it did not. `android/gradle.properties` now gives the daemon 3 GiB (and names the metaspace limit, which setting the arguments drops); Gradle reads it (`properties`). **Seen:** on `1714a0b` (that change and the documents over `a0b62a2`) the Android artifact run passed (44024996341): both kinds' native libraries, the checks, the split release APKs and the bundles, uploaded.

That derivation is now one, in `scripts/build_layout.py` (the SDK from `ANDROID_HOME`, `ANDROID_SDK_ROOT` or Gradle's `local.properties`; a value already set is kept); `dev.py` uses it and `./build.sh` evaluates it, and both refuse before building when no SDK is named. Tested (`scripts/test-dev.py`, a new case with a `local.properties` and with values already given); the local release run above used the same derivation (then still in `dev.py`); `./build.sh android` itself was not run again after the change, only `build_layout.py android`, which now prints the five values.

The application's plugin and store integration tests are Unix only (their fixture plugin speaks over Unix sockets) and were not run there; the owner cannot be started over SSH (E-136: the keyring refuses a network logon). **The window on Windows, seen:** the guest's console has a signed-in session, so a scheduled task run there (`/IT`) started the development window (debug build of `548b912`, `FREYA_RENDERER=software`: the guest has no GPU), which started its owner and the four extension interpreters in that session; the console was captured from libvirt (`virsh screenshot`) and driven through the guest's tablet and keyboard (QMP `input-send-event`, `send-key`). The window, its owner and the task were removed afterwards (the interpreters ended with the owner). **The store on Windows could never be reached** (Settings, Extensions: "The store could not complete that"; the owner's log: "The extension store could not be reached"), while the guest reached GitHub. The owner starts the download helper with an emptied environment, and Windows loads its network providers from the system folder that `SystemRoot` names: the helper alone, with the owner's request (`store-tree`), answered `network` with no environment and wrote the 7848-byte tree with `SystemRoot` only or with everything. `store_runtime.rs` now passes `SystemRoot` alone on Windows (the languages, fetched by the same helper, were cut off the same way). Rebuilt in the guest and **seen**: Refresh listed Test with the store badge; Remove ("Remove for good?") left its row in place, not installed, the Test page gone; Install showed the licence and the notice (Step 1 of 2, 2 of 2; `Cancel` left, `Next`, then `Back` and `Accept TOS` right), and after `Accept TOS` Test 0.1.2 ran again from the store with its page back. **Control's layout page failed on every opening there** ("A plugin could not complete the action"): it reads the screens (`peersus.displays` `inspect`), which needs the person's permission, and a refusal failed the page. Control now says on the page where to give it and reads again on its clock (`read_again`; a test with a refusal then an answer; Control 70 tests, Clippy clean); pushed to its submission as `35f824f`, the admission waiting for `007b9f2` cancelled.
- **macOS guest, on `548b912`:** the same host and interpreter tests pass, and the application's plugin (71, 1 ignored), store (15) and real-connection (25) integration tests pass. Three things had to change first:
  - **The native helper did not build on macOS** (known since the macOS evaluation): `cargo xtask native-host` passed `-pie` (unused under `-Werror` with Apple's compiler) and `-Wl,-z,relro,-z,now` (unknown to Apple's linker). Apple's toolchain links every program position independent and has no relocation-read-only options, so `xtask` leaves both out there; the test that compiles the helper and a native module did the same with the same flags and now passes on macOS (*a native library and a WASM module keep the same authority, in independent processes*). `stage-macos.py` now requires the helper with the other four programs instead of noting it absent. **Seen in the guest:** `cargo xtask build` exit 0 (release, 34 min; `ninja` must be on `PATH`), `peersus-native-host` a Mach-O x86-64 program, `stage-macos.py` staged `Peersus.app` with the five programs and `codesign --verify --deep --strict` accepted it.
  - **A test read `/proc`** to find the process an extension's named program runs as: on macOS it reads the system's list (`ps`, the name the kernel keeps and the executable it was started from); it passes there and on Linux.
  - **The test plugin wrote each log line in two writes** (`writeln!`): stopped between them by a restart, its line was joined to the next process's first ("write failed at 20000start 20421"), and a test never found what it waited for. One write per line now.
- **A phone's Settings: the group icons stood over the right edge of the page** (seen on NX in E-146: the edge of `Remove`, `Reset`, the interface size and the device name under them). The page now keeps that column free (`Scrolling`: the right padding is at least the icons' column and 2 px; `GroupMarks::CLEAR`). The 22 px it takes broke two translated words at the narrowest window (Dutch "Verwijderen", Swedish "Uppdateringar"): a row's value now takes at most half the row (was 55 %), and the picture's `Remove` is a one-line caption at the row's right end with one gap before it. New headless test at NX's width: nothing between the sheet's header and the tabs is drawn right of the icons' column; it fails on the old layout (`Remove`, `Reset`, `100 %`, the name). The word-break test passes in every language, now against the published 1108-message files (the ignored local copy still had 1072; refreshed from the `languages` branch, the old copy kept under `.backup/`). `peersus-ui` 733 pass, Clippy clean. **Seen on NX, build 22** (development APK, versionCode 23, installed over build 21 on NX and ROG and verified byte for byte, data kept; ROG not touched on screen): the cards of Overview, `Elimină`, the name field and `Resetează` end left of the icons, the size still 100 % after scrolling the page down past the slider.

Both now follow only the thread ids their own start added (one helper, `its_thread_ends_when_let_go`).

### Note of 2026-11-15: the second desktop did not show the pointer and its name tag, and kept closed windows on screen — the desktop's session, not the application; shown again after the owner logged in anew

- **Asked by the owner**, who also saw windows he had closed stay on that screen until he changed workspace (one of them an old Peersus window saying it had stopped, which he took for the running one).
- **Seen.** A picture taken through that desktop's own screenshot portal holds the closed windows: they are still in the compositor's scene. The service's probe run there (exit 0: the device and the drawn window were made) and four pictures taken meanwhile: in one, a desktop icon is lit as under a pointer, at the place the probe's pointer was due at that moment, and neither pointer nor tag is in the picture; that place is bare desktop, under no window. In the second the drawn window was mapped, the shell logged a failed promise in its handler for a mapped window (`_mapWindow`, `windowManager.js:1464`); in the second it was destroyed, the shell logged "too much recursion" with a trace alternating between two extensions.
- **Read there.** `burn-my-windows` (49) and `compiz-alike-magic-lamp-effect` (25) each replace the shell's `Main.wm._shouldAnimateActor` and keep what they found. The first calls what it kept whenever it has no effect of its own for the window; the second calls what it kept through a property it reads when called. In the trace each calls the other without end. A window the first has no effect for is therefore never answered for: a mapped one is not shown, a closed one is not removed. 74 such errors since 2026-11-14 10:45; the shell has run since 2026-11-13.
- **Inferred, not shown.** That the failed handler is why the pointer's window is not shown (how the shell completes a map was not read there: its own source was not found by the path tried). That each window left on screen is one of those errors.
- **Not tried.** The suspicion of 2026-11-14 (`xwayland-native-scaling`) was not tested and is not ruled out by a test of its own. Nothing of that desktop's settings or extensions was changed. Whether the application could show its pointer some way that does not pass through that function of the shell was not tried either: from what was read, a window the first extension has no effect for has no way round it, and the pointer is such a window.
- **After the owner logged in again (04:00).** He had turned `burn-my-windows` off (which, by itself and before the new login, changed nothing for him), logged out and in, and stopped another program of his own that he believes crossed with this one. The same probe, the same pictures: the pointer and the tag "Probe" are in every picture, at the places the probe's pointer was due; the new shell logged nothing while the pointer's window was shown and closed, and no recursion and no failed map handler since it started. The owner tested control from the workstation himself and found it right. `xwayland-native-scaling` was still on, at 166.67 %: that suspicion is gone. **Which of the three things cured it is not known**: the shell starting anew, the extension turned off and the other program stopped all came together.

### Notes of 2026-11-14 with no fix

- **The name tag on the second desktop is not seen.** Its overlay windows exist in that desktop's X server with the sizes of the names, a probe window reported viewable, and the owner saw nothing. That desktop runs GNOME 50.5 at 166.67 % with `xwayland-native-scaling`; the workstation (GNOME 51.1, no experimental feature) shows the tag. The feature as the cause is a suspicion: it was not turned off and tried.
- **A program started alone.** At 16:53 a bare development `peersus` (built that morning) was started from the file manager with no helper beside it: no window, every extension failed. `peersus stop` ended it. The owner did not say why its extensions fail in that case. **Changed:** at start it now names each helper program that is not beside it (`DesktopModules::missing`, one line on its error output, which is its log), and starts all the same, since the application without extensions is whole. One test; the lint is clean; not run from a folder without helpers, because a second owner beside the installed one would share its identity.

## Audit backlog (2026-11-10)

From a read-only source audit of the host, broker and interface (the interpreter, the Android adapters' lifecycle and the extension guests were **not** audited). Each row was found by reading code, not by running it: status is **OPEN — to be reproduced** until an entry above says otherwise. No P0 (data loss, security, crash) was found.

| ID | Sev | Finding | Where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A-01 | P1 | **Closed by E-009**; what it left (a control-frame write to an extension that never reads has no time limit: bounded, nobody else waits on it) is open. One subscriber with a full queue makes a published event fail for every subscriber; the host discards that error, and a control-frame write to an extension has no time limit | `peersus-plugin/src/hub.rs` publish, `link.rs`, `frame.rs`; `peersus-app/src/runtime.rs` publish call |
| A-02 | P1 | **Closed by E-011.** Android settings show two rows that only matter to optional extensions, with no extension installed; the search index names a third-party provider in common UI | `peersus-ui-android/src/lib.rs`, `peersus-ui/src/view.rs`, `settings_search.rs` |
| A-03 | P1 | **Closed by E-072.** The generic HTTP service allows one provider's request header by name and sizes a bound for one provider's protocol | `peersus-app/src/platform_services/file_http.rs` |
| A-04 | P2 | **Closed by E-069** (the shared queue of the host endpoint remains, bounded by each extension's rate). No per-extension rate limit or temporary suspension for calls, events, notifications; all extensions share the host endpoint's one call queue | `peersus-plugin/src/link.rs`, `hub.rs` |
| A-05 | P2 | **Closed by E-010.** An inbound stream refused before its call is queued leaves its entry in the receiving map | `peersus-app/src/runtime.rs` |
| A-06 | P2 | **Closed by E-071.** An extension with the inject grant can create virtual pointer devices without a cap (one per owner string) | `peersus-app/src/desktop_input.rs` |
| A-07 | P2 | **Closed by E-071.** One extension can hold all four consent-prompt slots for up to five minutes each | `extension_prompts.rs`, `platform_calls.rs` |
| A-08 | P2 | **Closed by E-071.** A persistent `accept()` error spins the broker's listener loop | `peersus-plugin/src/service.rs` |
| A-09 | P2 | **Closed by E-072.** Any grant, setting or peer change is published as one specific host service's event | `peersus-app/src/runtime.rs` |
| A-10 | P2 | **Closed by E-072.** The input service reads argument keys that only one consumer's protocol defines; two extensions declaring an input contribution are accepted and the first wins silently | `runtime.rs`, `desktop_input.rs`, `pass_through.rs`, `plugin_host.rs` |
| A-11 | P2 | **Decided in E-072: there is no grant between two extensions**, tested and written in the API page. Extension-to-extension calls route correctly, but no grant path can authorize a *guarded* method between two extensions, and no shipped extension consumes another | `hub.rs` permission, `runtime.rs` grants |
| A-12 | P2 | **Closed by E-012** (one table; the translations of consent words are not reviewed by speakers). Host permission wording exists in three lists; the interface's list lacks several declared permissions, which then show "No description was given" | `platform_services.rs`, `runtime.rs`, `view.rs` |
| A-13 | P3 | **Answered by E-071**: the queue an extension can fill was already bounded; places were not merged in it, and are now. Captured-input queues are unbounded while an extension is slow to answer | `surfaces.rs`, `input_follow.rs`, `desktop_capture.rs` |
| A-14 | P3 | Duplicates: the find-or-add-import block (×3), the API-key validity rule (×2), the handshake timeout (×2), an identifier charset; an element-tree byte limit larger than the payload that can carry it | `runtime.rs`, `model.rs`, `desktop_services.rs`, `link.rs`, `data.rs`, `private_store.rs`, `elements.rs` |
| A-15 | — | **Closed by E-055.** Modules that need each other (crates cannot form a cycle; modules can). The first count of 2026-11-14 was wrong: it followed `crate::` paths written in comments and missed the grouped form `use crate::{a, b}`, so it named two pairs that only mention each other in a doc comment (`peersus-api` `contributions` ↔ `elements`, `peersus-ui` `avatar` ↔ `avatar_file`) and missed `peersus-net` `reconnect` ↔ `transport`. Counted again without comments and test modules there were three pairs and three groups; all are gone and the count is a step of the gate | `scripts/module-cycles.py` |

Its six "layer violations" are matches of a name (`say`) between unrelated files and are not real | `/tmp` output of the analyzer, not kept in the tree |

## Decisions for the owner

- **Commit policy and fixtures (E-002).** Exempting temporary directories in the workstation policy would restore six local tests. It is the owner's policy to change; nothing here changes it.
- **`workspace_member` (E-004).** Either remove the key from the validator and the README (every extension is an independent workspace, as `control` already is, and `clipboard`/`upload` drop the line), or define which SDK members may be overlaid. The first matches the current architecture; it changes the submission contract, so it is not done silently.
- **Counting allocations.** The measuring tools report CPU, memory, context switches, disk bytes and frame stages, not allocations: a counting allocator is an `unsafe impl`, which the workspace forbids outside the two Android crates, and this machine has no heap profiler installed. Either accept a development-only dependency that wraps the allocator safely for the measuring example alone, or install a heap profiler on the workstation and measure from outside. Until one is chosen, allocation counts stay unmeasured and are said so in every report.
- **Sampling profiler.** `samply 0.13.1` is installed, but the workstation's `kernel.perf_event_paranoid` is 2 and it refuses to sample as an ordinary user (tried on `demo_window`; nothing recorded). Lowering it to 1 is a system setting that needs root; it is the owner's to change. Until then a hot path is found by the probe's stage times and by narrowing a workload, not by a sampled call tree.
- **Element-tree limit (A-14).** `peersus_api::elements::MAX_BYTES` says a tree may be 128 KiB, but a tree reaches the application as an extension's answer to a call, and every operation an extension sends, an answer included, is refused by the broker above 48 KiB as a whole message (`link.rs` hands each one to `hub.rs` `submit`, whose first check after liveness is `message::bounded`); the frame that carries it is at most 64 KiB. So no tree above about 48 KiB can arrive. Read in source; not tried with a large tree. The constant is part of the published contract: either lower it to what a message carries (`MAX_PAYLOAD`, in the same crate), or raise the carrier. Not changed.
- **Request headers of the HTTPS service (A-03): DECIDED and built, E-072**, under the authority the owner gave on 2026-11-15. The headers are named by the extension when it asks to send a file, and shown to the person with the file and the address: the kind of (a) that does not change the manifest. What was weighed before: `platform_services/file_http.rs` allowed an extension two request headers by name, `content-type` and one that only one provider's protocol uses; any `x-` header would also admit the ones proxies act on, a fixed prefix would have to be accepted by the provider, and leaving it was a recorded exception.
- **The cost of controlling (E-019, E-023).** What is left is spread over the whole path of a batch of moves, sixty times a second, and each way of lowering it changes something a person or an extension sees: (a) fewer batches a second from the page's pad, which lowers every stage on both devices and makes the pointer less even; (b) a path for moves that does not pass through the extension's interpreter as text for each batch, which is a change of the extension contract and must stay usable by any extension; (c) a device's own pointer drawn otherwise than as a window that is moved (7–9 % of a core on GNOME while it moves), which changes how that pointer is made and where it can be seen; (d) accept the measured cost. A package that is not debuggable has also not been measured on a phone, and may by itself lower the system's share there. None was taken.

## Not validated

Everything not marked VERIFIED, OBSERVED or MEASURED in an entry above, and nothing beyond what that entry says was looked at. In particular, as of 2026-11-17 (the current state of each row is kept in the [Go/No-Go evaluation](go-no-go.md)):

- **ROG:** carries the development build of E-147 (installed and verified byte for byte, data kept), and the stress run and the presence of the other devices were read there through the control channel (E-074, E-066); its screen has not been looked at since E-017.
- **Windows:** a virtual machine only. The window was seen on its console with the software renderer and an extension's page driven (E-147); two transfer tests of many files run out of their time on the guest's slow disk (E-024); no installer, and the application cannot update itself there.
- **macOS:** a virtual machine only (x86-64, no Metal: the software renderer). It builds, `cargo xtask build` included, its tests pass and the window was seen (E-147, [macOS evaluation](macos-evaluation.md)); the bundle is sealed ad hoc, not notarized; nothing on a real Mac or on Apple Silicon.
- **Optimized builds:** the Linux release programs are installed on both desktops (Go/No-Go row 1); the Android release packages were built here (E-147) but never installed (no release signer), and CI's Android artifact fails in packaging; the Flatpak and AppImage that CI produces were not installed or started from CI's copy.
- **Updates:** the Play path, the refusals on Android without a provider, a rollback against a real release, and the check on its own clock (E-029).
- **Extensions under stress:** on NX, ROG and one desktop, not on HP, with nothing passing between devices meanwhile (E-028, E-074).
- **Performance:** no allocation counts; the cost of controlling is measured and its options open (E-019, E-023, E-146).
- **Looked at by a person:** the screens in languages other than English, beyond what the layout test lays out.
